### [1] [An SYK wormhole under the microscope](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.20699)
*Valérie Bettaque,Martin Sasieta,Brian Swingle,Alejandro Vilar López*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: 本文在Sachdev-Ye-Kitaev (SYK)模型中推导出一个大N虫洞鞍点，用于描述重算符热单点函数跨微观耦合实现的方差，并提出该鞍点对应双喇叭几何。在双标度极限下，虫洞被表示为连接两个边界的弦在涌现体希尔伯特空间中的跃迁振幅，并在Schwarzian极限下与Jackiw-Teitelboim (JT)引力的开放宇宙希尔伯特空间建立联系。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 研究动机是在SYK模型中理解重算符热单点函数在不同微观耦合实现下的方差行为，并通过推导虫洞鞍点来检验SYK模型中的重算符随机性假设。此外，该研究旨在建立SYK模型与JT引力理论之间的对应关系，深化对全息对偶和量子引力的理解。

Method: 采用大N展开方法在SYK模型中推导虫洞鞍点配置；提出该鞍点描述双喇叭几何结构；在双标度极限下，将虫洞表示为涌现体希尔伯特空间中"弦"（连接两个边界的关联子）的跃迁振幅；在Schwarzian极限下，弦增殖并定义JT引力开放宇宙希尔伯特空间中的量子跃迁振幅；通过比较SYK虫洞振幅与耦合物质的JT引力中对应振幅来检验重算符随机性假设；计算固定耦合实现下α态中的可观测量及半虫洞的体波函数；将体描述扩展到闭合宇宙的希尔伯特空间。

Result: 成功推导出SYK模型中重算符热单点函数方差的大N虫洞鞍点；确定该鞍点对应双喇叭几何；建立了虫洞与涌现体希尔伯特空间中弦跃迁振幅的对应关系；在Schwarzian极限下，将虫洞与JT引力的量子跃迁振幅联系起来；给出了半虫洞的体波函数，该波函数在长度基底下表现出不自平均的不规则关联；发现虫洞几何涌现的关联弦在结构上类似于局域观测者的世界线；为直接检验SYK中重算符随机性假设提供了理论框架。

Conclusion: 本研究在SYK模型中成功推导并刻画了虫洞鞍点的微观起源，将其与双喇叭几何及涌现体希尔伯特空间中的弦结构联系起来。在Schwarzian极限下，建立了与JT引力理论的明确对应关系，为检验重算符随机性假设提供了直接途径。微观SYK耦合决定虫洞的α参数，体波函数展现出不自平均的不规则关联特性。研究进一步将体描述扩展到闭合宇宙希尔伯特空间，并指出关联弦与局域观测者世界线的结构相似性，深化了对全息对偶、虫洞几何和量子引力的理解。

Abstract: We derive a large-N wormhole saddle in the Sachdev-Ye-Kitaev (SYK) model for the variance of the thermal one-point function of a heavy operator across realizations of the microscopic couplings. We propose that this saddle describes a double trumpet geometry. In the double-scaling limit, we express the wormhole as a transition amplitude in an emergent bulk Hilbert space of chords connecting the two boundaries. In the Schwarzian limit, the chords proliferate and the wormhole defines a quantum transition amplitude in the open-universe Hilbert space of Jackiw-Teitelboim (JT) gravity. Comparing the SYK wormhole amplitude with its counterpart in JT gravity coupled to matter provides a direct test of the heavy-operator randomness hypothesis in SYK.
  The microscopic SYK couplings specify the α-parameters of the wormhole. For a fixed realization of the couplings, we compute the observable in the corresponding α-state. We provide the bulk wavefunction of the associated half-wormhole, which exhibits erratic correlations in the length basis that do not self-average. We extend the bulk description to the Hilbert space of a closed universe. We point out that the correlation chords from which the wormhole geometry emerges structurally resemble local observer worldlines.

</details>


### [2] [Region bounds, error margins and certified positivity in the numerical conformal bootstrap; Or the first mathematically rigorous bounds on the 3d Ising CFT critical exponents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.20701)
*Marten Reehorst*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: 本文建立了数值共形自举界的完全严格化框架,填补了采样点、有理逼近、有限自旋列表和浮点舍入四个缺口,并通过精确算术和机器可验证证明,在3d Ising模型中给出了临界指数η和ν的严格双边界。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 现有的数值共形自举界虽被称为"严格",但实际上存在四个根本性缺陷:仅在采样点而非参数空间区域上成立、使用截断的有理逼近而非真实共形块、仅考虑有限自旋列表、以及依赖浮点数舍入。这些缺陷使得已发表的结果无法构成真正的数学定理,需要建立完整的严格化框架。

Method: 提出四项关键技术解决方案:(1)开发高效搜索方法在参数空间区域(包括外部维度空间)上寻找正定泛函;(2)将大自旋交叉向量的正定性作为区域约束施加;(3)利用共形块递推关系的新解析界,证明泛函在真实未截断交叉向量上的正定性;(4)通过精确算术验证泛函正定性。技术实现包括:三变量Bernstein基正定性证明、Q(√2)上的精确算术、无穷自旋尾部的紧化处理、真实块与截断之间差异的显式界,将无穷多正定性条件归约为有限多个机器可验证的符号条件。

Result: 在(Δ_σ, Δ_ε)平面上验证了排除区域:对于Δ_σ∈[0.505, 0.5405],除包含3d Ising岛的小矩形外,Δ_ε∈(1/2, 3]全部被排除,在明确的谱假设下给出临界指数η和ν的严格双边界。证明了混合σ-ε系统的解析输入,可移除自旋2间隙假设。区域界方法将岛的映射简化为少量一维二分搜索,提高了自举搜索效率并防止常见失效模式。所有核心代码和机器可验证证明已发布。

Conclusion: 本文首次建立了数值共形自举的完全严格化数学框架,通过精确算术、解析界和机器可验证证明填补了所有理论缺口,使共形自举结果真正成为数学定理。在3d Ising模型应用中展示了方法的有效性,为临界现象研究提供了严格的数学基础。该框架不仅提高了结果的可靠性,还改善了计算效率,为后续研究提供了可复现的工具和方法。

Abstract: Numerical conformal bootstrap bounds are commonly called rigorous, yet as published they are not theorems: they hold at sampled points rather than on regions of parameter space, for truncated rational approximations of the conformal blocks, for a finite list of spins, and up to floating-point rounding. We close all four gaps. We show how to search efficiently for functionals positive on regions, including external-dimension space; how positivity on the large-spin crossing vectors can be imposed as such a region constraint; how new analytic bounds on the conformal-block recursion relations give functionals positive on the true, untruncated crossing vectors; and how to certify the positivity of these functionals in exact arithmetic. The certification rests on Bernstein-basis positivity certificates in up to three variables, exact arithmetic in $\mathbb{Q}(\sqrt2)$, a compactified treatment of the infinite spin tail, and an explicit bound on the difference between the true blocks and their truncations, so that infinitely many positivity conditions reduce to finitely many machine-checked sign conditions. As a flagship application we certify an exclusion region in the $(Δ_σ,Δ_ε)$ plane: for $Δ_σ\in[0.505,0.5405]$ all of $Δ_ε\in(1/2,3]$ is excluded except a small rectangle containing the 3d Ising island, giving rigorous two-sided bounds on the critical exponents $η$ and $ν$ under explicitly stated spectrum assumptions. The analytic inputs for the mixed $σ$-$ε$ system, which would remove the spin-2 gap assumption, are also proved. The same methods prevent common practical failure modes and can make bootstrap searches faster: region bounds turn the mapping of an island into a handful of one-dimensional bisections. The essential code to reproduce the search and the certification, and all machine-checkable certificates, are released.

</details>


### [3] [Local supersymmetry implies momentum map/Gauntlett-Pakis equations and vanishing generalized Komar forms](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.20931)

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: 本文通过局部超对称代数在玻色场上的作用，直接推导出Killing旋量双线性满足的一阶微分方程（包括动量映射方程、Killing矢量方程和超对称Killing矢量的广义Komar荷消失条件），并在多个复杂度递增的例子（包括11维超引力）中验证了这一方法。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 传统上，Killing旋量双线性满足的一阶微分方程（如动量映射方程、Gauntlett-Pakis方程等）的推导过程较为复杂。本文旨在寻找一种更直接、更系统的方法，通过局部超对称代数在玻色场上的作用来推导这些方程，从而揭示这些方程的代数起源，并在超引力理论中验证这一方法的普适性。

Method: 本文采用局部超对称代数作用于玻色场的代数方法。具体而言，通过分析超对称变换在玻色场上的作用，直接从局部超对称代数的对易关系中提取出Killing旋量双线性所满足的一阶微分方程。该方法在多个复杂度递增的超引力理论例子中进行了应用和验证，其中最复杂的例子是11维超引力理论。

Result: 通过局部超对称代数的作用，成功推导出了Killing旋量双线性满足的完整一阶微分方程组，包括动量映射方程（或Gauntlett-Pakis方程）、Killing矢量方程以及超对称Killing矢量的广义Komar荷消失条件。这些结果在包括11维超引力在内的多个超引力理论例子中得到了验证，表明该方法具有普适性和有效性。

Conclusion: 本文证明了Killing旋量双线性满足的一阶微分方程可以直接从局部超对称代数在玻色场上的作用中推导出来，这为理解这些方程的代数结构提供了新的视角。该方法在11维超引力等复杂理论中的成功应用表明，这是一种系统且强大的推导工具，对超引力理论中超对称解的研究具有重要意义。

Abstract: The first-order differential equations satisfied by Killing spinor bilinears (momentum map equations or Gauntlett--Pakis equations, Killing vector equations and the vanishing of the generalized Komar charge of the supersymmetric Killing vector) can be derived directly from the action of the local supersymmetry algebra on bosonic fields. We show how this comes about in several examples of increasing complexity, one of which is 11-dimensional supergravity.

</details>

<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 探索二维dilaton引力理论中规范场论描述与几何描述之间的内在联系,特别是在离散路径积分框架下理解这两种描述如何相互关联。目标是阐明具有相应测度的几何Regge路径积分如何从其规范理论形式中自然涌现出来。

Result: 成功证明了在欧几里得符号且宇宙学常数为零或负值的情况下,带状BF理论的离散化通过适当的规范固定可以导出Regge dilaton引力的表述形式。建立了规范理论离散化与几何Regge离散化之间的明确对应关系。

Conclusion: 本研究明确揭示了二维dilaton引力的几何Regge路径积分及其相关测度如何从规范理论表述中涌现。这种对应关系为理解引力的规范-几何对偶性提供了离散层面的具体实现,加深了对二维dilaton引力量子化的理解,并为相关理论的数值研究和进一步推广奠定了基础。

Abstract: In this work we investigate the relation between gauge and geometric descriptions of two-dimensional dilaton gravity at the level of discrete path integrals. We develop a Regge discretization of Euclidean two-dimensional dilaton gravity using homogeneously curved simplices, as well as a ribbon refined discretization of two-dimensional BF theory adapted to homogeneously curved spacetimes. For Euclidean signature and vanishing or negative cosmological constant we show how the ribbon BF discretization, through appropriate gauge fixings, leads to the Regge dilaton gravity formulation. The resulting correspondence makes explicit how a geometric Regge path integral with its associated measure emerges from its gauge theoretic formulation.

</details>


### [5] [Stability and dynamics of bumblebee models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21066)
*Quentin G. Bailey,Robertus Potting,Dario T. Walter-Cardona*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: 本文研究了闵可夫斯基时空中具有非导数势V(X)的矢量场理论(bumblebee模型)中的自发洛伦兹对称性破缺,分析了哈密顿量的稳定性、线性化扰动谱、球对称解以及柯西初值问题的适定性。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 标准二次势的哈密顿量从下方无界导致不稳定性,需要研究光滑势函数(包括纯三次势)是否能实现稳定的自发洛伦兹对称性破缺,并验证该模型在经典场论层面的数学一致性和物理可行性。

Method: 采用哈密顿形式分析方法,确认光滑势函数的哈密顿密度有界性和全局极小值;推导时间型情况下的哈密顿运动方程并验证其在全局极小值处的正则性;对全局极小值附近的小扰动进行线性化谱分析;通过解析和数值方法研究球对称特解的时间演化;识别场方程失去双曲性的场组态子空间。

Result: 证实包括纯三次势在内的一类光滑势函数具有从下方有界的哈密顿密度,在满足X=b²>0的非零类时矢量场期望值处达到全局极小值;哈密顿运动方程在全局极小值处是正则的;光滑初始组态的时间演化可发展出类激波奇点;发现了场方程不再是双曲型的场组态子空间,导致柯西初值问题适定性的破坏。

Conclusion: 具有光滑非导数势(如纯三次势)的bumblebee模型能够实现稳定的自发洛伦兹对称性破缺,其哈密顿量从下方有界且在全局极小值处正则。然而,该模型存在内在的数学病理:光滑初始条件可演化出激波奇点,且在某些场组态区域场方程失去双曲性,破坏了柯西问题的适定性,这对模型的物理可行性提出了挑战。

Abstract: In this work, we study vector field theories with spontaneous Lorentz violation induced by a nonderivative potential $V(X)$ (with $X = B_μB^μ$), known as bumblebee models, with a Maxwell kinetic term in Minkowski spacetime. We confirm a recent result showing that a class of smooth potentials, including a purely cubic potential, leads to a Hamiltonian density that is bounded from below and assumes a global minimum for nonzero timelike expectation values of the vector field satisfying $X = b^2 > 0$, inducing spontaneous Lorentz-symmetry breaking. We show explicitly that the standard quadratic potential has a Hamiltonian that is unbounded from below and thus unstable. We analyze the Hamiltonian equations of motion for the timelike case and demonstrate that they are regular at the global minimum. Furthermore, we examine the linearized spectrum of small fluctuations around the minimum. We also study particular solutions with spherical symmetry analytically and numerically, without assuming linearization. We find that the time evolution of smooth initial configurations can develop shock-like singularities, and we explain why this behavior is expected in this model. We also identify a subset of the space of field configurations in which the field equations cease to be hyperbolic, leading to a breakdown of well-posedness of the Cauchy initial-value problem.

</details>


### [6] [Difference between pair creation and anomaly matching: Nontrivial aspects of invertible symmetries and defects in $SU(N)$ models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21478)
*Yoshiki Fukusumi,Shuma Nakashiba*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: 本文研究了SU(N)_K Wess-Zumino-Witten模型之间的联系,通过拓扑量子场论中的(半)整数自旋简单流和共形场论中的反常匹配条件,建立了强配对条件K=±R²K'(mod N)和弱反常匹配条件K=±RUK'(mod N),揭示了配对条件在关联对称性与缺陷方面的规范作用。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 研究目标是理解具有未破缺Z_N对称性的SU(N)_K WZW模型与SU(N)_K' WZW模型之间的内在联系,特别是探讨拓扑量子场论中的简单流条件与共形场论中反常匹配条件之间的关系,以及它们对对称性和缺陷守恒的影响。

Method: 采用两种理论框架进行分析:1) 在拓扑量子场论框架下,利用(半)整数自旋简单流条件分析折叠模型SU(N)_K⊠_{Z_N}SU(N)_K'的性质,推导广义配对条件;2) 在共形场论框架下,应用反常匹配条件建立模型参数之间的关系;3) 比较两种方法得到的条件差异,分析其物理意义和理论一致性。

Result: 研究获得两个关键结果:1) 从(半)整数自旋简单流条件得到强配对条件K=±R²K'(mod N),其中gcd(R,N)=1;2) 从反常匹配条件得到较弱条件K=±RUK'(mod N),其中gcd(R,N)=gcd(U,N)=1。重要发现是,仅满足反常匹配而不满足配对条件会导致Z_N缺陷和对称性守恒定律的内在不匹配。

Conclusion: 配对条件能够规范地关联对称性与缺陷(如范畴论中的对应关系),比单纯的反常匹配条件更强且更具物理一致性。然而,配对条件能否作为禁止无质量重整化群流的指导原则仍不明确,这为后续研究留下了开放性问题。研究揭示了拓扑和共形场论在处理对称性结构时的微妙差异。

Abstract: We study the connection between the $SU(N)_{K}$ Wess-Zumino-Witten (WZW) model and $SU(N)_{K'}$ WZW models, enforcing the unbroken $\mathbb{Z}_{N}$ symmetries. The fundamental underlying structures are the (half-)integer spin simple currents in the topological quantum field theories and anomaly matching conditions in conformal field theories. For the (half-)integer spin simple current condition of the folded models $SU(N)_{K}\boxtimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{N}} \overline{SU(N)_{K'}}$ or $SU(N)_{K}\boxtimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{N}} SU(N)_{K'}$, we obtain the generalized pairing condition $K= \pm R^{2}K' (\text{mod}.N)$ with $\text{gcd}(R,N)=1$. On the other hand, the anomaly matching condition results in the weaker condition $K= \pm RUK' (\text{mod}.N)$ with $\text{gcd}(R,N)=\text{gcd}(U,N)=1$. Surprisingly, the anomaly matching without the pairing condition results in the intrinsic mismatch of the preservation law of the $\mathbb{Z}_{N}$ defects and symmetries. The pairing condition canonically relates symmetries and defects, as in category theory, whereas it is unclear whether it can be a guiding principle in prohibiting the massless renormalization group flows.

</details>


### [7] [Krylov complexity and oscillatory quantum dynamics in two-field inflation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21574)
*Tao Li,Lei-Hua Liu,Hai-Bing Fu*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: 本文研究了双场暴胀中原初扰动的Krylov空间动力学，通过求解耦合曲率和熵扰动的模方程，构建了四模压缩态描述的量子态，分析了两个压缩通道的Krylov复杂度演化，发现两通道在视界穿越时表现出不同的起始时间和振荡动力学特征。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>

Starting from a pure product state, we analytically determine the time evolution of entanglement entropy, antiflatness, capacity of entanglement, and nonlocal trace distance magic under unitary dynamics generated by random matrix Hamiltonians drawn from all three Gaussian ensembles. We find that the transient maxima of antiflatness, capacity of entanglement, and nonlocal magic occur on the same characteristic timescale as the maximum rate of entanglement growth. These results establish that the magic barrier is a universal feature of RMT thermalization, suggesting universality across quantum chaotic systems. We conclude that quantitative simulations of thermalization in ergodic quantum theories inherently require quantum computation.

Abstract: Developing mechanisms to control transport in correlated quantum systems is essential for advancing quantum circuit technologies. In this work, we establish a rectification mechanism for both particle and energy flow in a fermionic system coupled to external baths, in the presence of a tilted potential modulated locally by a time-periodic drive. The drive generates particle and energy currents even in the absence of chemical or thermal bias between the baths. These drive-induced currents are enhanced at resonant frequencies at which avoided crossings occur in the Floquet quasienergy spectrum, reflecting the role of the Floquet states as intermediate channels for transport. Once a bias between the baths is introduced, the drive-induced current contribution can reinforce, compete with, or even cancel the bias-induced contribution. These effects enable rectification in the non-interacting regime, which we quantify via the rectification coefficient $\mathcal{R}$. This coefficient measures the asymmetry between transport up the tilt (forward bias) and down the tilt (reverse bias). In addition to standard rectification, where transport is reduced under reverse bias, we identify a regime of bipolar rectification, in which the current flows only up the tilt regardless of the bias polarity. At the boundary between standard and bipolar regimes, the system achieves perfect rectification, corresponding to complete suppression of the reverse-bias current. In the presence of interactions between particles, the parameter region that supports bipolar rectification is enlarged and $\mathcal{R}$ can be enhanced by many orders of magnitude. Our results establish a rectification mechanism for particle and energy transport that can be experimentally realized in condensed matter and cold atom systems.

Abstract: Quantum imaginary-time evolution (QITE) provides a systematic route to ground-state preparation, but its cost grows rapidly with the locality $D$ of the unitary approximation, for which the local Pauli basis scales as $4^D$. We introduce Sparse-QITE, a regularized formulation that promotes compact generators, reducing the number of quantum operations and yielding circuits with fewer gates and lower depth. We derive rigorous bounds on the sparsification error and benchmark the method in one-dimensional transverse-field Ising and antiferromagnetic Heisenberg models. Sparse-QITE exhibits a controllable sparsity--accuracy trade-off while retaining strong compression even in the high-accuracy regime. In scalability experiments with respect to $D$, the active support grows much more slowly than in standard QITE and, in several regimes, approximately saturates over the domain sizes studied. After circuit construction and transpilation, some of the largest-domain cases require only about $1\%$ of the standard-QITE gate count while maintaining high accuracy. We further introduce adaptive and active-discovery variants: the former selects the regularization strength dynamically during the evolution, while the latter reduces the number of Pauli expectation values that need to be evaluated and, consequently, the measurement requirements. These results establish sparsity-promoting regularization as a route toward more resource-efficient QITE. More broadly, these results suggest that sparsity-promoting regularization may also benefit variational quantum algorithms.

Abstract: Determining whether quantum states can be interconverted by a prescribed set of transformations is a fundamental problem in quantum information. Here we develop a general classification framework based on conserved operators (COs), i.e., not necessarily hermitian matrices having the state of interest as an eigenstate. We first show that COs belonging to the algebra generating the allowed transformations can be obtained from the kernel of a quantum covariance matrix. Their number determines the dimension of the accessible orbit, while their algebraic structure and eigenvalues provide further constraints on state convertibility. We establish general bounds on the number of COs and prove that highest-weight states, like separable states in the case of $n$ qudits, attain the maximum. We also show how these operators generate parent Hamiltonians having the characterized state as an exact ground state. We then also consider positive zero-eigenvalue COs, which encode forbidden directions of reduced density matrices and incorporate rank-based constraints into the same framework. Applying this approach to multiqubit SLOCC, we obtain complementary classifications from algebraic and rank COs. While for $n\leq 3$ qubits both lead to the usual classes, for $n=4$ qubits they distinguish 17 and 20 types separately, and 29 when combined. The combined classification yields 287 fixed types and $4$ continuous families for $n=5$ qubits, and 4926 fixed types and 147 continuous families for $n=6$ qubits, providing, to our knowledge, the most refined exhaustive classification of SLOCC-convertibility classes based on such data.

Conclusion: 通过引入反对易索引和利用局域性原理，monoprop突破了传统算符传播方法的O(KN)复杂度瓶颈，实现了反直觉的O(K/N)扩展性能。该框架的大规模并行能力和配套的代理图优化方法，为处理大规模量子化学模拟和自适应量子电路优化提供了高效工具。这一开源框架有望成为量子算法研究和应用中算符传播计算的新标准工具。

Abstract: We introduce monoprop, an open-source, massively parallel operator-propagation framework for fermion and qubit bases. For $N$ qubits or modes, existing codes represent each of the $K$ tracked operator terms (Pauli strings or products of Majorana operators) as a $2N$-bit string, testing each for anticommutation with the gate at $O(KN)$ cost per gate. Here, we introduce the anticommutation index which rapidly looks up the terms anticommuting with a gate. A local gate changes only the terms that touch its sites, and when terms are truncated to act on few sites, only a fraction $\sim1/N$ touch any given qubit or mode, and we use this to prove the time per gate scales as $O(K/N)$. This counterintuitive result tells us that for a fixed number of terms, increasing the number of sites can make things faster. We demonstrate this numerically, and show monoprop scales almost ideally to 8192 cores, tracking $9.5\times10^{10}$ terms. Beyond propagation, we present memory-efficient surrogate graphs, which record one propagation and replay it at new angles, for fast energy re-estimation and parameter gradients, and an efficient method for finding energy-minimising gates and optimal angles for simulating adaptive quantum-chemistry circuits.

Abstract: Drone-delivery planning combines routing decisions with operational constraints such as payload capacity and limited onboard energy, resulting in combinatorial optimization problems that can become costly to encode directly in binary form. This work investigates a hybrid quantum--classical approach in which candidate drone missions are generated and evaluated classically before constructing a quadratic unconstrained binary optimization (QUBO) model for mission selection. Payload- and energy-infeasible missions are removed during preprocessing, while the reduced QUBO is solved using exact enumeration, simulated annealing (SA), and the Quantum Approximate Optimization Algorithm (QAOA), with mixed-integer linear programming (MILP) used as a classical reference. Experiments on controlled synthetic delivery instances evaluate solution quality, the effect of preprocessing, and computational scaling. Exact QUBO optimization reproduces the MILP optimum on the evaluated small instances, while SA achieves $100\%$ feasibility with a median optimality gap of $0\%$. Combined payload-and-energy preprocessing reduces the mean number of QUBO variables by $29.60\%$ and quadratic interactions by $49.20\%$, while reducing mean SA runtime by $31.26\%$. In contrast, the evaluated shallow statevector-based QAOA configuration achieves lower feasibility and is restricted to small QUBO instances. These results indicate that, in the considered setting, the main practical benefit of the hybrid architecture comes from classical feasibility preprocessing that reduces the binary search space before QUBO-based optimization.

Abstract: Quantum collapse (QC) occurs under the action of the inverse-power potential U(r)=-U_{0}/(2r^{n}) in the framework of the Schroedinger equation. The corresponding stationary wave functions feature an infinite number of spatial oscillations at $r\rightarrow 0$. The ground state (GS) is missing in the presence of the QC, but the available bound states at finite energies may be physically relevant, as they are normalizable. This work aims to produce singular stationary wave functions. The analytical findings represent an asymptotic form of the stationary wave functions at $r\rightarrow 0$, which fill a continuous spectrum, and some exact solutions for the wave functions, obtained under appropriate conditions. As a regularized version, we also consider the potential truncated at a finite difference from the center. The regularized wave functions feature a finite number of spatial oscillations, the missing GS reappears, and the spectrum of the bound states is discrete.

Abstract: Progress in quantum hardware requires a growing volume of experiments across diverse devices, straining the capacity of human experts. Automation remains challenging because device differences and drift can render workflows developed on one processor unsuitable for another. Developed on one processor and applied sequentially to four others, the strategy uses an agent to assess experimental evidence, select subsequent measurements and adapt procedures, with operators providing instrument preparation and physical intervention. Using three-qubit Grover state preparation as a common task, each deployment combined device-specific calibration, control-pulse optimization and measurement. The experimental strategy was refined through sequential deployment, and all four processors ultimately produced outputs dominated by their respective marked states. The marked-state populations of the reconstructed dominant eigenstates ranged from \(0.910\) to \(0.959\), with relative deviations below \(3.8\%\) from the ideal value of 0.945. These results demonstrate that an agent-assisted experimental strategy can be reused and refined across processors to realize a common quantum task, providing a basis for scaling experimental practice beyond individual devices.

Abstract: Recent studies have reported promising results from quantum machine learning in drug discovery, healthcare, materials science and cybersecurity. Controlled angular-polynomial experiments distinguish targets adequately represented by a single view from those that depend on complementary angular and polynomial relationships. Controlled classification tests further show that gains depend on alignment between the chosen representation and the target, and on a suitable fusion rule. Benchmark validation shows improved mean predictive performance over classical baselines on Wine, Wisconsin Diagnostic Breast Cancer, three-class Fashion-MNIST and Steel Plates Faults within the evaluated protocols. Classical replacement and masking controls remain competitive, classical baselines lead on Digits and Covertype, and outcomes with missing or corrupted views depend on the available inputs. These findings support evaluating quantum representations for their complementary predictive contribution and matching the representation and fusion strategy to the target relationships.

Abstract: Long-range, genuine multipartite entanglement (GME) between distant parties in a quantum network is a crucial resource for distributed quantum information processing. Through controlled manipulation of short-range GME states in the network via stochastic local operations aided by classical communication (SLOCC), long-range GME can be established between selected target nodes on demand. Abstract: Tests of gravity-mediated entanglement aim to determine whether two massive objects in spatial superposition can become entangled through their mutual gravitational interaction. The results identify finite-width and electrostatic tolerance boundaries that should be considered in future tests of gravity-mediated entanglement.

Abstract: We investigate the Casimir force on thin suspended dielectric layers covered with graphene, both theoretically and experimentally. Thermal effects of the Casimir force on graphene occur at separations significantly smaller compared to ordinary metals and dielectrics. At such small separations, the Casimir force is sufficiently strong to be measured experimentally. However, the presence of substrates to support the graphene significantly reduces the enhancement. We show theoretically that replacing the solid substrate beneath graphene with a thin dielectric membrane partially restores this strong thermal dependence. One of the interacting objects is a solid gold plate. The other object is a suspended dielectric membrane made of either 15 nm of silicon oxide or 13 nm silicon nitride, covered with graphene. At a separation of $\sim$500 nm, theoretical predictions yield changes in the Casimir pressure of up to 54 % and 40 % for silicon oxide and silicon nitride, respectively, when the temperature is increased from 10 K to 294 K. Measurements were performed for graphene on a suspended silicon nitride membrane at room temperature, using a gold-coated sphere attached to a cantilever as the force sensor. The measured force gradient is consistent with the theoretical prediction, although the thermal dependence could not be resolved, in part because of uncertainties in the optical properties of silicon nitride. Replacing a bulk substrate with a thin dielectric membrane covered with graphene provides a substantial reduction of the Casimir force, which may facilitate the stable operation of neutral nanomechanical elements at small separations.

Abstract: Which features of a quantum state are needed for a relativistic quantum field theory to emerge from a lattice system? Our results establish two complementary parts of this relationship. As an explicit example, we prove that for stabilizer systems with short-range order, the local algebras are isomorphic to tensor products of hyperfinite factors of type I or type II. This demonstrates that the absence of magic is accompanied by the absence of type III factors in the local algebras, therefore prohibiting the appearance of a quantum field theory. Second, for the hyperfinite factors of Araki, Woods and Powers, we prove a quantitative equivalence: The limiting factor is of type III if and only if the locally optimized min-relative entropy of magic diverges. This divergence is necessary to generate the continuous spectrum associated to a type III factor. Thus, in this setting, the emergence of a type III factor requires not merely the presence of magic, but an infinite amount of it. These results provide a rigorous setting supporting the proposal that infinite magic is a necessary structural ingredient for the emergence of a relativistic continuum from lattice systems.

These results establish periodic conditional measurements as a general framework for engineering conditional quantum states and manipulating event-detection statistics in monitored quantum dynamics.

The result allows block splitting, regrouping, nonzero-syndrome selection, and adaptive linear reconciliation.

Previous work reduced this cost by restricting the trainable phases to predefined sparse patterns, such as a diagonal or a grid, which however may not be optimal for a specific target state. In this work, we address this limitation by introducing a method that learns which phases are needed together with their values, using a differentiable mask. A sparsity penalty encourages the removal of unnecessary phases, while temperature annealing gradually turns the masks into binary decisions. We compare the proposed method with fully parameterized and predefined sparse ansatzes on Fourier and Haar-random target states. The evaluation considers both the minimum number of phases required to reach different infidelity thresholds and the overall trade-off between fidelity and phases required, measured through Pareto frontiers and hypervolume. The results show that learning the location of the phases together with their values provides competitive or improved fidelity--resource trade-offs, although the advantage depends on the target state and qudit dimension.

Abstract: Robust quantum control usually assumes one of two extremes: the error is of a known form and only its strength is unknown, or nothing at all is known about it. In practice one often sits in between. The size of a static error is unknown, but its class is known, for example, it only shifts energy levels, or it acts on each subsystem separately. We ask how fast a control can cancel, to first order, every error in such a class. A time-independent Hamiltonian can do this if and only if all errors in the class have zero diagonal in a common basis. In that case an equally spaced energy ladder in this basis does it after one period. A lower bound that holds for any time-dependent control with bounded drive power shows that this simple constant control is optimal for several natural classes, including unknown level shifts and unknown local errors on two subsystems. Completely unknown errors need a time-dependent control, and there, knowing the error class shortens the minimum time by a factor $\sqrt{d^2+1}$. So partial knowledge of the error changes not only how long an optimal robust control takes, but also its form.

Abstract: Reliable characterization of quantum states and quantum processes is central to the development of scalable quantum computing. Standard characterization techniques such as full quantum state tomography become rapidly impractical as system size increases. In this work we present two software-oriented fidelity estimation protocols that avoid full tomographic reconstruction while remaining experimentally relevant for noisy intermediate-scale quantum devices. The first protocol is direct fidelity estimation (DFE), where the fidelity between a target state and an experimentally prepared state is estimated from importance-sampled Pauli measurements. The second protocol is a survival-probability-based method that reconstructs fidelity from experimentally accessible moments of a noisy evolution and its approximate inverse, and is intrinsically robust against state-preparation-and-measurement (SPAM) errors. We discuss the theoretical basis of both approaches, analyze their noise sensitivity, and present numerical evidence on representative multiqubit states under depolarizing, dephasing, and amplitude-damping channels. For DFE we show that attenuation-aware correction improves robustness against measurement noise. For the survival-probability approach we show that an $L_2$-optimized expansion substantially outperforms naive Taylor truncation in high-noise regimes.

Abstract: Driven-dissipative collective spin systems can host several long-lived metastable states; rare switching between them governs slow relaxation and first-order dissipative phase transitions associated with changes in the dominant stationary state. Here we develop its numerical implementation, combining collocation and shooting methods, and apply it to two microscopically motivated systems: the Lipkin-Meshkov-Glick model with purely collective dissipation and the cooperative resonance fluorescence with collective and local dissipation. By determining the leading exponential scaling of switching rates between metastable attractors, we predict the stationary magnetization, the first-order dissipative phase-transition point, and the exponential scaling of slow relaxation rates. These predictions agree with quantum master-equation calculations and an exact analytical result for the second model without dephasing. We also examine how the choice of quasiprobability representation affects numerical convergence and find that the alternative semiclassical Wigner approach, based on Gaussian truncation, has varying accuracy across models and parameter regimes. These results substantiate the framework's applicability to experimentally relevant collective spin systems with both collective and local dissipation.

Abstract: High-order harmonic generation from molecules is highly sensitive to molecular geometry and strong-field electron dynamics, providing a powerful route for probing and controlling ultrafast processes in extended systems. Real-time time-dependent density functional theory simulations are performed with the molecular axis aligned parallel to the laser polarization direction. The results show that increasing the molecular chain length significantly enhances the harmonic yield and tends to extend the cutoff region. In particular, C$_{10}$H$_2$ exhibits much stronger plateau harmonics than C$_2$H$_2$, while the intermediate molecule C$_6$H$_2$ shows harmonic intensities between those of C$_2$H$_2$ and C$_{10}$H$_2$. Time-frequency analysis and charge-density evolution reveal that the enhanced HHG emission in longer chains originates from the enlarged electron acceleration distance and increased probability of multi-center recollision, especially near the terminal carbon atoms. These findings demonstrate that molecular-size engineering offers an effective strategy for controlling harmonic emission and optimizing attosecond light sources in extended molecular systems.

Abstract: Finite rate randomness expansion (RE) which is secure against quantum adversaries is a crucial information processing task which is well studied in the case of two non-communicating parties sharing Bell non-local correlations in a device independent manner. In this paper we show that contextual correlations with only a single localized party are sufficient for RE in a semi-device independent (SDI) manner. We propose a template SDI-RE protocol which is applicable for arbitrary $N$-cycle non-contextuality (NC) inequalities and derive its RE rate under finite statistics. As a special case, we also highlight the advantages of the situation when RE is constrained by Clauser-Horne-Shimony-Holt (CHSH) inequality but with characterized measurements implemented by only a single party. Our template protocol also incorporates non-ideal statistics which may not satisfy perfect commutativity and perfect repeatability, both of which are necessary in a loophole-free test of NC inequalities. As such, several recent experimental implementations of significantly loophole-free tests of NC inequalities can be modified for secure RE following our approach.

Method: 通过几何分析证明单量子比特串行电路存在"相位锁定"现象:由于其状态位于布洛赫球面上,所有参数在每个输入处只能沿一个复方向移动状态,导致最多只有2E+2个切向方向,仅比所需的2E+1个傅里叶系数多一个;而双量子比特对照电路通常至少有6E+6个切向方向。通过100次独立运行实验,在固定步数预算内比较两种电路达到最优解的成功率,使用三种优化器(梯度下降、Adam、Levenberg-Marquardt)和多个数据集(平谱目标的精确损失和三个真实世界序列)进行测试。

Result: 在编码预算E=32的平谱目标任务中,串行电路在梯度下降、Adam和Levenberg-Marquardt优化器下的收敛成功率分别为3/100、53/100和83/100,而对照电路为99/100、100/100和100/100。在三个真实世界序列任务中,匹配参数数量时,串行电路在Adam下最多9次成功、在Levenberg-Marquardt下32-93次成功,对照电路分别至少98次和全部100次成功。串行电路在Adam下的留出误差约为对照电路的1.5倍,但在Levenberg-Marquardt的典型运行中无显著差异。

Abstract: Angle-encoded variational quantum circuits implement truncated Fourier series. For one-dimensional inputs, a single-qubit re-uploading (serial) circuit represents every function that a multi-qubit circuit with the same encoding budget $E$ can; we find that this exchange of width for depth is not free in first-order training. Because its state lives on the Bloch sphere, all parameters of the serial circuit move the state, at each input, along one complex direction, which we call \emph{phase-locking}. We prove that this costs no Fourier direction but leaves no direction to spare: however many parameters it has, the serial circuit has at most $2E+2$ tangent directions, one more than its $2E+1$ Fourier coefficients need, while a two-qubit control with the same encoding budget and model class generically has at least $6E+6$. The cost in training is measured against this control, as the number of runs out of 100 that reach the optimum within a fixed step budget. On an exact loss with flat-spectrum targets the gap grows with $E$; at $E=32$ the serial circuit converges in 3, 53 and 83 runs under gradient descent, Adam and Levenberg--Marquardt, the control in 99, 100 and 100. On three real-world series, at matched parameter counts, the serial circuit reaches the least-squares optimum in at most 9 runs under Adam and in 32 to 93 under Levenberg--Marquardt, the control in at least 98 and in all 100; its held-out error is up to about $1.5$ times that of the control under Adam, but no larger in the typical run under Levenberg--Marquardt.

</details>

Abstract: Riemannian optimization provides a natural geometric framework for designing quantum algorithms for ground-state preparation, most notably through quantum imaginary-time evolution. However, existing geometry-aware approaches primarily employ steepest-descent updates, leaving more advanced methods from classical Riemannian optimization largely unexplored. In this work, we introduce quantum conjugate gradient descent (QCGD), a quantum-compatible implementation of Riemannian conjugate gradient that combines the steepest descent direction with information retained from previous search directions. In our numerical experiments, the proposed approach achieves faster convergence per iteration than the baseline double-bracket quantum imaginary-time evolution (DB-QITE) algorithm, and we quantify the associated circuit cost with gate counts produced by Qrisp. This improvement, however, comes at the cost of increased circuit depth for each update step, revealing a trade-off between convergence rate and implementation complexity. Nevertheless, since the circuit depth for those algorithms grows exponentially with the number of iterations, the proposed method may offer practical advantages, particularly in the early fault-tolerant regime.

</details>

Abstract: We determine the finite-horizon identification limits of a \(d\)-dimensional memory when the control interface is restricted to four shared, time-homogeneous generators. A writer encodes a message by applying a prefix word to a common root state. The verifier then applies a candidate-dependent query word followed by a shared terminal binary test, with each word of length at most \(T\). For fixed error tolerances \(λ_2>0\) and \(λ_1+λ_2<1\), the optimal identification sizes satisfy \(\ln N_{\mathsf Q,4}=Θ_{λ_1,λ_2}(\min\{d^2,T\})\) for arbitrary CPTP generators and \(\ln N_{\mathsf S,4}=Θ_{λ_1,λ_2}(\min\{d,T\})\) for stochastic maps. Four unitary letters and four permutation letters attain these respective bounds, showing that a four-letter reversible alphabet reaches the static code-size orders in optimal quadratic and linear word length. In the fully dimension-limited regime, this yields \(\ln N_{\mathsf Q,4}=Θ(d^2)\) versus \(\ln N_{\mathsf S,4}=Θ(d)\), establishing a quadratic quantum--classical separation in logarithmic code size. Low-overlap Grassmann and agreement-code packings supply the orbit endpoints, while exact inverse query words match each encoded state to its designated test. At the sharp boundary \(λ_2=0\), \(0\leqλ_1<1\), all four model classes satisfy \(\ln N=Θ(\min\{\ln d,T\})\). Thus any fixed positive false-acceptance tolerance activates the metric-packing regime, whereas zero false acceptance imposes an exclusive-witness constraint and yields the logarithmic-dimension boundary.

  <summary>Details</summary>

We benchmark ground-state energies, fidelities, superposition degrees and required expansion lengths against exact diagonalization and equivalent SCI schemes for the transverse-field Ising, XXZ Heisenberg, and Hubbard models, finding particularly strong compression from the stabilizer representation in the spin models, and increasing advantage with system size for these systems. This method provides a general benchmark for continuous-wave sources in interference-based quantum network protocols.

Abstract: The quantum Buridan's ass problem concerns a network of interconnected nodes in which transitions between the nodes are governed by the laws of quantum mechanics. We have in mind a quantum analogy to changing an opinion, in which the initially occupied set of nodes is one opinion bubble, with the remaining parts of the network corresponding to a different opinion. We find that quantum interference again plays a crucial role in the dynamics. A further key component is the role of symmetry between the nodes and their interconnections, as it is symmetry that lies at the heart of the Buridan's ass puzzle. In the classical Buridan's ass paradox it is symmetry between competing alternatives that freezes the ass into indecision. In the quantum case, however, interference results in a far richer dynamics.

Abstract: Protecting quantum batteries from environmental noise remains a challenge. A finite superadditive region persists at fixed RTN amplitude, with protocol-dependent location and gain. In the Ising model, the enhancement is nonmonotonic in DM strength, with intermediate coupling giving near-optimal performance. Dressed-channel analysis associates this enhancement with changes in transition frequencies and longitudinal-noise matrix elements that reduce slow-RTN spectral weight. As an application of the $XX-YY$ interaction, we also demonstrate two-spin squeezing beyond the standard quantum limit through two-axis countertwisting. The existing studies have considered the single-mode squeezed vacuum for difference-intensity and parity detection, and the two-mode squeezed thermal state for parity detection alone as the initial Gaussian resources. However, a real squeezer in the lab actually produces single-mode squeezed thermal light, which has not been studied in a coherent-plus-resource interferometer. We take up this case and using the phase space Wigner picture, we obtain the heralded state produced by photon subtraction, addition and catalysis on a single-mode squeezed thermal resource, and evaluate the corresponding phase sensitivities. Single-photon catalysis, which is useless on squeezed vacuum, becomes a useful operation for any thermal occupation above $\nth\simeq0.2$, and since it heralds with a probability near one-half, it turns out to be the best success-weighted operation over the whole thermal range. In the vacuum limit our results reproduce the known hierarchy, where two-photon catalysis is the best operation. The quantum Fisher information computations confirms that thermal noise enhances the phase information encoded in the catalyzed state, demonstrating that difference-intensity detection closely approaches the ultimate precision bound across most operational regimes, and, together with a loss analysis, verifies the robustness of the effect.

Abstract: In this work we study the application of two-photon correlated quantum states for high-precision time estimation. To achieve this, photon pairs were fed into a Hong-Ou-Mandel polarization interferometer to generate N00N entangled states with $N = 2$. Two- and four-detector schemes were considered, and the achievable time estimation precision of each was studied, with particular emphasis on the system's Fisher information in each case. Our results reveal that using a four-detector scheme, enabling the measurement of both coincidence and photon-bunching terms, achieves the maximum precision given by the quantum Fisher information, without the need of characterizing system losses. We also show that correlations between repetitions arise due to systematic errors, limiting the precision enhancement that can be obtained by increasing the number of repetitions. Taking these correlations into account, we show that the four-detector scheme outperforms the two-detector scheme in the precision of time-delay estimation. With the experimentally determined calibration uncertainties, the four-detector scheme improves the dispersion by a factor of 2 for the repetitions performed, reaching up to a factor of 4 as both schemes approach their systematic floors.

Abstract: We examine how a finite many-body system can function as an effective charger for a single-qubit quantum battery, using the Lipkin-Meshkov-Glick model as a paradigmatic setting. As our figure of merit, we consider the battery ergotropy while explicitly resolving the energetic flows across the composite system. In the absence of intra-charger interactions, we show that the generation of non-zero ergotropy is controlled by the competition between collective coupling and spectral mismatch. We further demonstrate that even passive positive-temperature charger states can generate finite battery ergotropy, provided the charger is sufficiently large and sufficiently hot. Intra-charger interactions restructure the charger spectrum and qualitatively modify the available energetic pathways. While they can enhance the amount of energy accessible for charging, they can simultaneously divert energy into internal many-body and battery-charger interaction channels, producing an energetic impedance to direct charger-to-battery transfer. Consequently, battery ergotropy alone is insufficient to characterize an effective many-body charger: the useful work generated and its energetic provenance must be assessed together.

We derive necessary and conditional sufficient bounds on low-body approximation accuracy using standard operator complexity measures and characterize possible dynamical failure mechanisms of the state-adapted method. Numerical benchmarks demonstrate substantial state-dependent accuracy gains, while identifying regimes of reduced accuracy or violations of Pauli spectral bounds. For a three-dimensional system of $80$ interacting spins, our method achieves strong body-order convergence, close agreement with independently converging tensor-network simulations, and substantially smaller sparse Pauli representations. These results establish state-adapted Pauli propagation as a promising approach to classical simulation of higher-dimensional coherent many-body dynamics.

Abstract: On a chain of exchange-coupled quantum dots a Heisenberg exchange pulse of area pi is a SWAP, so the parallel bubble-sort SWAP network of superconducting chains is native to exchange-only (EO) spin qubits, and any other pulse area fuses routing with an entangling interaction. We build a variational "bubble-sort exchange network" (odd-even transposition, one parameter per pulse) and study it as a Born machine read out by Pauli spin blockade. Exchange conserves total spin, so the reachable readout distributions are fixed by the total-spin content of the initial product state. For the two-spin singlet-triplet encoding we derive a selection rule -- outcomes with k triplet pairs are bounded by the initial weight in sectors S <= k (S = 0 excluded for k = 1) -- and prove by a transportation argument that it is also sufficient: the reachable distributions form the polytope cut out by the rule, and its total-variation floor is attained. Products of singlets and T0's forbid whole outcome classes; the Neel state forbids none; the three-spin EO code has no floor. We then distil, in noise-free simulation, next-token distributions of GPT-2 over restricted vocabularies into the network, with matrix-product-state (MPS) Born machines as classical control. At six logical qubits the three-spin chain reaches KL = 0.011 nat at depth N and 0.004 at 2N, below the bond-dimension-4 MPS; the two-spin chain from the Neel state reaches 0.014 at 2N and 0.004 at 3N, against 0.28-1.5 from singlet/triplet products for the first teacher. Final states lie largely outside the code space: leakage is a resource, not an error. No arrangement of disjoint pulses meets every pair in fewer than N-1 rounds, and at depth 2N the chain distils as well as a 2D array or non-local couplings. Sector-restricted simulation extends this to 10-14 logical qubits (up to 30 dots).

Abstract: We introduce patchwork, a lattice-surgery compiler for arbitrary multi-qubit Pauli measurements on a prescribed narrow-corridor two-dimensional surface-code layout. Systematic geometric rules simplify stabilizer placement and gate scheduling, including for logical $Y$ measurements implemented using twist defects. We introduce measurement circuits and schedules that, together with protruding lobes of $Y$-extension patches, preserve the full effective code distance for all numerically tested code distances. To support parallel decoding, we develop a wavefront algorithm that partitions merged lattice-surgery patches into space-time commit, buffer, and cleanup regions. Simulations of three sequential multi-qubit Pauli measurements acting on ten logical qubits show that staged decoding achieves total failure probabilities close to those of monolithic decoding over the parameters tested. These methods provide an automated framework for fault-tolerant lattice surgery and a route toward scalable, real-time decoding.

Abstract: Assuming only the solubility of Kirchhoff's laws, the conservation of energy, and the form of the constitutive relations, we present a flux-charge symmetric Hamiltonian formalism that always admits a symplectic reduction for nonsingular, well-posed circuits involving gyrators, inductors, and capacitors (LCG circuits) with arbitrary non-linearity. The formalism introduces a notion of circuit duality on non-linear non-reciprocal circuits and a family of point transformations that inserts, removes, and moves reciprocal elements across gyrators while preserving the Hamiltonian spectrum. With these transformations, we extend Tellegen's classic result that gyrators make reciprocal elements behave as their dual to non-linear reciprocal elements such as the Josephson junction and quantum phase slip. Furthermore, we present conditions under which non-reciprocal circuits can be transformed to dynamically equivalent reciprocal circuits.

Abstract: Greenberger-Horne-Zeilinger (GHZ) states are multipartite entangled quantum states that play a central role in quantum information theory, with applications in quantum communication, cryptography, and distributed quantum computing. A major experimental goal is to create high-dimensional GHZ states using multiple entangled parties. Krenn, Gu and Zeilinger discovered a surprising bridge between these quantum-optical experiments and graph theory: certain photonic experiments can be represented by edge-coloured, edge-weighted graphs, and the dimension of the produced GHZ state equals the number of feasible monochromatic inherited vertex colourings. This led Krenn and Gu to conjecture that every GHZ graph with more than four vertices has dimension at most $2$. A counterexample would give a new high-dimensional multipartite GHZ construction, while a proof would clarify fundamental resource limitations in quantum optics. It is known that the conjecture holds for graphs of vertex connectivity at most $2$ [MFCS 2024], and that simple GHZ graphs with $n>4$ vertices have dimension less than $n/\sqrt{2}$ [Quantum 2024]. However, no general upper bound depending only on vertex connectivity was known. We prove that every simple connected GHZ graph $G$ with at least four vertices has dimension at most $2^{κ(G)-1}+\binom{κ(G)}{2}$, where $κ(G)$ is the vertex connectivity of $G$. We also prove new bounds in terms of edge connectivity and maximum degree.

</details>

Abstract: Semiconductor spin qubits offer a promising route to compact and scalable quantum computers, combining long coherence times in isotopically purified silicon with compatibility with semiconductor manufacturing. Beyond computational speedup, their energy efficiency is an important consideration for future large-scale systems. We evaluate the power and energy requirements of a Loss-DiVincenzo spin-qubit quantum computer by analyzing the Quantum Fourier Transform (QFT) and benchmarking it against the classical Fast Fourier Transform (FFT). We develop a detailed energy budget, identifying cryogenic cooling as the dominant baseline contribution before quantum error correction (QEC). The comparison between the QFT and the classical FFT reveals that, for any quantum workload operating under a polynomially bounded sampling regime, the crossover in energy consumption occurs at smaller problem sizes than the crossover in execution time. Thus, quantum computing can become energetically competitive before achieving a speedup in execution time. We then incorporate surface-code QEC and analyze the energy costs of QEC cycles, gate synthesis, and decoding. At scale, instrumentation and decoding become dominant contributions, while cryogenic cooling imposes a constraint on scalability. We show that optimizing the control-signal demultiplexing ratio can reduce the overall energy cost by approximately two orders of magnitude. For a system with about one million physical qubits, we estimate a power consumption of approximately 100 kW, at least one order of magnitude below that of today's highest-performance classical computing systems.

Abstract: We investigate the fate of quantum many-body scars in the PXP model subject to Markovian dissipation. We show that when local jump operators respect the same symmetries of the Hamiltonian the full Liouvillian spectrum displays a tower of states with properties analogous to standard quantum scars: large overlaps with Néel states and equally spaced gaps in the imaginary part of the eigenvalues, set by a single frequency. Whether the long-time dynamics is dominated by scars is determined by the real parts of the eigenvalues, which are set by the ratio between the two relaxation rates $γ_{\pm}$ describing spin flips down-to-up or up-to-down respectively. For asymmetric dissipation ($γ_+ > γ_-$) the scarred eigenvalues have the smallest real parts: the resulting enhanced fidelity oscillations are further stabilized by staggered jump operators. In contrast, replacing the full projection operator in the dissipators with a partial one breaks the symmetries of the problem and is seen to destroy dissipative scars altogether.

Abstract: Fault-tolerant quantum computing (FTQC) introduces substantial systems-level complexity through quantum error correction (QEC), making debugging FTQC programs challenging. Existing quantum debugging approaches primarily target logical-level circuits and do not naturally support debugging within realistic FTQC execution flows. We present SynDebug, the first systems-level debugging framework for FTQC programs. SynDebug introduces lightweight assertion-based debugging primitives that operate directly within encoded FTQC infrastructures while avoiding additional logical surface area and minimizing temporal overhead. We evaluate SynDebug across multiple QEC code families, code distances, and encoded workloads, including state preparation, entanglement validation, and Shor-style programs. Our results demonstrate that SynDebug enables practical FTQC debugging with an average 1.8% additional circuit depth overhead (0% in most cases) and no measurable increase in the logical error rate, showing that systems-level debugging for large-scale FTQC applications is both feasible and practical.

Abstract: The classical capacity of a noisy optical channel is the maximum rate at which information can be transmitted reliably, given a constraint on the mean input energy. For the ubiquitous bosonic Gaussian channels, which include standard models of loss and noise in fibre and free-space optical links, determining this rate requires addressing whether entanglement between channel uses or non-Gaussian signals can outperform Gaussian encodings. More precisely, the question is whether the energy-constrained Holevo information is additive across arbitrarily many channel uses and coincides with its Gaussian counterpart. We answer these fundamental questions in the affirmative for every single-mode bosonic Gaussian channel at every input energy. Namely, we prove that independently prepared Gaussian states with Gaussian-distributed random displacements achieve the capacity, including in the low-energy regime where squeezing the signals competes with encoding information in their displacement. Our proof connects an entanglement inequality to a sharp, additive lower bound on output entropy. We then characterise communication above capacity: under a strict total photon-number cutoff, the probability of correct decoding decreases exponentially with the number of channel uses, and we derive explicit bounds on the decay exponent. When only the mean photon number is constrained, a simple energy-rescaling formula determines exactly the entire asymptotic rate--error trade-off: the capacity at mean photon number $N$ and allowed average decoding error $ε$ equals the vanishing-error capacity at mean photon number $N/(1-ε)$. Together, these results identify the ultimate limits of classical communication for some of the most fundamental models of optical communication.

</details>

Abstract: Classical simulation of magic state factories, including distillation, cultivation, and code switching, is challenging because of their substantial non-Clifford content. We introduce $\texttt{magna}$ (magic-state-factory assessment via global network algebra), a classical simulator that simulates such protocols as cheaply as Clifford circuits. $\texttt{magna}$ expresses the figures of merit of a protocol, namely the acceptance rate and the fidelity of the accepted states, as tensor networks, which it reduces globally by exploiting a structural correspondence with transversal gates on quantum codes. $\texttt{magna}$ further supports feedforward conditioned on arbitrary functions of mid-circuit measurement outcomes, including decoder outputs. We simulate Bravyi--Haah distillation with up to 100 output magic states at a cost that does not grow exponentially in the number of outputs, and achieve state-of-the-art sampling times for distance-5 magic state cultivation on both color codes and RP$^2$ surface codes. Finally, we validate nonlinear feedforward on a logical-level distillation protocol with 15 rounds of decoder-dependent feedforward. $\texttt{magna}$ aims to contribute to the key infrastructure for the large-scale evaluation and optimization of magic state factories.

  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 量子电路的噪声信道具有多种Kraus表示,对应不同的unraveling(纯态系综),虽然它们给出相同的密度矩阵和可观测量期望值,但各轨迹携带的量子资源(特别是非稳定性magic)不同。由于stabilizer方法的经典模拟成本随magic指数增长,研究如何通过优化unraveling选择来降低轨迹magic,对提升含噪量子动力学的经典模拟效率具有重要意义。

The canonical unraveling in terms of Pauli jumps is Clifford and leaves magic untouched, so that trajectories retain the Haar-random value of non-stabilizerness. The optimal unraveling reduces the extensive magic density of the trajectories by a $p$-dependent factor without altering its linear scaling with system size. At the same time, it produces a very broad distribution of magic over trajectories, with a sizable fraction of trajectories retaining Haar-random magic. Since the cost of stabilizer-based classical simulation grows exponentially with the magic of the state, these high-magic trajectories, rather than the average, control the simulation cost. Our results delineate the potential and the limitations of unraveling optimization as a route to the classical simulation of noisy quantum dynamics with stabilizer methods.

We prove that, for the normalized code-projector state of a $\mathbb{Z}_d$ qLDPC stabilizer code under local Pauli noise, the CMI across a buffer $B$ separating $A$ from $C$ decays exponentially with the width of $B$ in two complementary noise regimes independent of system size. At weak noise, this holds for full-system tripartitions, where correlations between the two sides require an error cluster to cross the buffer, an exponentially unlikely event. At strong noise, exponential decay holds whenever the buffer supports no logical operator, since correlations can persist only along rare chains of buffer qudits that remain informative. Under an additional space-uniformity assumption, we establish the robustness of Levin--Wen topological entropy. On finite-dimensional lattices of size $n$, the weak-noise bound further implies a constant-layer quasi-local Petz recovery channel of range $O(\poly(\log n)/\varepsilon)$ that recovers the noiseless state within trace-distance error $\varepsilon$. Both CMI bounds apply to general qLDPC interaction graphs, including geometries where such constant-depth quasi-local recovery need not exist. Thus, the exponential decay of the noise-induced CMI provides a general criterion for mixed-state phase stability.

Abstract: Significant efforts are currently being directed toward the implementation of large-scale quantum information processors, but realizing useful quantum advantage also requires the development of efficient quantum algorithms. Here we introduce an evolutionary framework in which large language models (LLMs) propose circuit designs and an independent evaluator verifies and scores them. The first law holds in both independent directions of the resulting two-parameter solution space. We express the mass as a function of entropy and angular momentum, recover the geometric thermodynamic potentials, and establish a scale-corrected Smarr relation that retains the dimensionful coupling. The exact metric defines the geometry, while equilibrium thermodynamics is consistently restricted to second order in spin.

Abstract: Hydrodynamic modeling of viscous neutron stars requires a choice of theory of relativistic hydrodynamics. That distinct formulations contain the same physical content in the long-wavelength limit is referred to as ``hydrodynamic universality.'' It has been recently shown that hydrodynamic universality can break down in radial neutron star oscillations: causality and finite-size effects restrict access to the long-wavelength universal hydrodynamic regime in sufficiently viscous stars. We show that this breakdown occurs for a broad range of causal hydrodynamic frames in Bemfica--Disconzi--Noronha--Kovtun (BDNK) theory. Increasing viscosity collectively pushes this class of causal frames away from the BDNK regime of validity, eventually forcing all to lie outside it at bulk viscosities in the range $ζ\sim10^{30}$--$10^{31}\mathrm{g}/\mathrm{cm}/\mathrm{s}$. This breakdown of universality is accompanied by material changes in the oscillation mode structure: non-hydrodynamic modes become damped on timescales comparable to those of hydrodynamic modes, the first overtone can become longer lived than the fundamental mode, and unphysical instabilities with sub-millisecond timescales can emerge. Even in regimes where universality has broken down, we find that the inviscid threshold of gravitational collapse remains unchanged in BDNK theory. These results provide evidence that first-order theories of relativistic hydrodynamics may be insufficient for modeling sufficiently viscous neutron stars.

Abstract: In analytically tractable models of Einstein-scalar critical collapse, it has been shown that the quantum self-energy of the collapsing matter in the dominant $s$-wave sector grows with Lyapunov exponent $D-2$, eventually competing with the classical instability that controls black hole formation. At large $D$, the horizon tracing reduces to a Bessel problem, yielding a quantum wall whose radius obeys $r_h\propto(D-2)^{-2}$. This radius can remain above the Planck scale at finite $D$, although maintaining this hierarchy becomes increasingly demanding as $D$ grows. We construct a covariant one-loop action whose stress tensor remains smooth throughout the DSS domain and organize it into an echo-periodic structure without memory. Abstract: Near the threshold between scattering and capture by a Schwarzschild black hole, a particle spends a long time close to an unstable circular orbit, which in the ultrarelativistic limit approaches the light ring. Here we study the behavior of the gravitational wave fluxes emitted by such an ultrarelativistic particle at first order in the mass ratio. The fluxes radiated to infinity and absorbed by the horizon diverge as the inverse distance from the light ring multiplied by its logarithm. Using the Wentzel, Kramers, and Brillouin (WKB) approximation to solve the Regge-Wheeler equation and treating the infinite harmonic sum analytically, we derive an exact expression for the coefficient of this logarithmic enhancement and show that it is identical for both fluxes. We then estimate the coefficients of the subleading pole contributions by combining numerical calculations at low multipoles with the exact asymptotics at large multipoles. We further conjecture an extension to equatorial circular orbits in Kerr. Interestingly, our results also inform the analytical structure of the energy and angular momentum losses of ultrarelativistic scattering orbits near the light ring. More broadly, our work illustrates how WKB methods can provide the asymptotic behavior of self-force observables in regimes where numerical mode sums become increasingly challenging.

Abstract: This thesis maps two exotic black-hole geometries onto the transonic flow of a de Laval nozzle and reads their perturbative response in aerodynamic variables. The first is the hairy black hole of gravitational decoupling, constrained by the dominant energy condition and by the existence of a horizon. The second is the Calmet-Kuipers metric, short-ranged and governed by the single Wilson coefficient of the dimension-six operator that the two-loop Goroff-Sagnotti divergence identifies. Perturbations of spin 0, 1/2, 1 and 2 reduce to a Schrödinger-type wave equation, whose spectrum the Mashhoon method returns as a whole overtone tower in closed form, every frequency damped. The two corrections are distinguished by which channel responds and by how strongly all of them do, the quantum coupling moving the quality factor by up to 37% in the scalar and Dirac channels and by about 10% in the other two, while the hair acts on every channel alike. Identifying the two tortoise coordinates turns the equality of the potentials into a Sturm-Liouville problem, so that the prescribed barrier fixes the wall profile and with it the flow variables and the thrust coefficient. The hair is felt across the whole divergent section, whereas the quantum correction registers only near the throat and grows as the inverse fourth power of the mass, which confines it to primordial black holes. The analogy is kinematical, so the duct reproduces the scattering of perturbations off these geometries without reproducing the field equations behind them.

Abstract: We study the bound states perturbations in the interior of the Schwarzschild black hole and examine whether or not their microstates spectrum can furnish a statistical description of the black hole entropy. These states contain a discrete set of modes characterized by the labels $(s, p,\ell,m)$, where $s$ represents the spin of the species, $p$ denotes the radial excitation number while $(\ell,m)$ are the usual angular momentum labels. Requiring that the shortest wavelength resolved by the highly excited bound states remains above the quantum gravity scale introduces an angular cutoff $\ell_{\rm max}\sim r_H/L_{\rm Pl}$. The resulting angular degeneracy therefore scales with the horizon area. We count the corresponding configurations in the microcanonical ensemble and find an entropy of the form $S_{\rm bs}=αA/\hbar G$. Demanding that the bound state sector to be in thermal equilibrium with the black hole at the Hawking temperature fixes the fraction of the bound state energy in total black hole energy. For the gravitational perturbations sector, the equilibrium bound state contribution in entropy and the energy fraction are typically small. We also examine the conditions under which the bound states could account for the full black hole entropy. Although such saturation is possible at the level of state counting, it requires an order unity fraction in the black hole energy and therefore the backreactions on the background geometry cannot be ignored.

</details>

  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 现有的Lorentz对称性自发破缺理论(如大黄蜂引力)中缺乏精确的正则黑洞和虫洞解,同时需要理解这些修正引力理论中Simpson-Visser几何族的性质、观测特征(如阴影和引力透镜效应)以及稳定性问题,特别是探测场扰动和可能的梯度不稳定性对理论可行性的约束。

Method: 1) 在度规大黄蜂引力框架下,利用幻影标量场、非线性电动力学源和类空固定模矢量场构造精确的Simpson-Visser度规解;2) 通过独立矢量方程确定局部拉格朗日乘子和时间分量,保证度规视界处的正则性;3) 重构物质场函数,识别虫洞、零最小几何和黑-反弹的连续参数族;4) 计算光子球、临界轨道、光线偏折、近日点进动等观测量,并与Schwarzschild解和Sgr A*、M87*观测数据对比;5) 推导耦合轴对称扰动方程,区分张量、度规和横向大黄蜂模式特征;6) 采用Chebyshev谱配置法数值计算无质量标量场和Maxwell探测场的基本准正则模共振频率,并用几何光学方法估计张量和大黄蜂模式共振尺度;7) 通过势能非负性分析排除指数增长模式,并用变分方法构造负非最小耦合情形下电磁场超常极化的不稳定增长模式及其增长率下界。

Result: 1) 成功构造了度规大黄蜂引力中的精确Simpson-Visser解族,包含虫洞、黑洞和黑-反弹几何;2) 黑洞情形下,面积光子球半径和临界类时轨道半径保持Schwarzschild值,但角偏折、近日点进动和光子轨道不稳定性依赖于反弹参数和大黄蜂参数;3) 固定失效质量和面积观测者位置时,度规阴影与Schwarzschild预言一致,Sgr A*阴影直径和M87*角质量观测无法单独确定这些参数;4) 轴对称扰动方程显示出不同的张量、度规和横向大黄蜂特征;5) 数值计算得到无质量标量场和Maxwell场的准正则模共振频率;6) 非负探测势排除了指数增长模式;7) 但在负非最小耦合和非零时间分量情形下,电磁场的超常极化出现角向梯度不稳定性,该不稳定特征在耦合主符号中持续存在,变分方法确认了固定几何上的增长模式及其增长率下界。

Conclusion: 本文在度规大黄蜂引力理论中成功构造了包含虫洞和黑洞的精确Simpson-Visser解族,该解由幻影标量、非线性电动力学和类空矢量场支持。虽然某些经典观测量(如阴影)与Schwarzschild黑洞一致,使得现有观测数据无法有效约束大黄蜂参数,但理论预言的角偏折和轨道特征仍依赖于修正参数。关键的稳定性分析揭示,在负非最小耦合情形下,支持电磁场会出现超常极化的角向梯度不稳定性,这对该理论框架的物理可行性构成重要约束,表明需要进一步限制参数空间或修正耦合形式以避免不稳定性。

Abstract: We construct an exact Simpson-Visser solution in metric bumblebee gravity, supported by a phantom scalar, magnetic nonlinear electrodynamics, and a spacelike configuration of fixed norm. The independent vector equation determines a local Lagrange multiplier and a temporal component that permits a regular ingoing extension across metric horizons. Reconstruction of the matter functions identifies a continuous family of wormholes, null minimum geometries, and black-bounces within one action. In the black hole case, the areal photon sphere and critical timelike orbit radii retain their Schwarzschild values, whereas angular deflection, periapsis advance, and photon orbit instability depend on the bounce and bumblebee parameters. The metric shadow coincides with the Schwarzschild prediction at fixed lapse mass and areal observer position; in addition, the adopted Sgr A* shadow diameter and M87* angular mass comparisons do not determine these parameters separately. The coupled axial equations yield distinct tensor, metric, and transverse bumblebee characteristics. We compute fundamental auxiliary massless scalar and ordinary Maxwell probe resonances by Chebyshev spectral collocation and estimate the tensor and bumblebee resonance scales at leading geometric optics order. Nonnegative probe potentials exclude exponentially growing probe modes. For negative nonminimal coupling and a nonzero temporal case, however, the supporting electromagnetic field develops an angular gradient instability in its extraordinary polarization. The unstable characteristic persists in the coupled principal symbol, while a variational construction establishes growing modes on the fixed geometry and lower bounds on their growth rates.
